Free Tier Model Names (Production + Preview)\n\n| Model ID | Parameters | Status | Best For |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `gpt-oss-120b` | 120B | Production | Coding, reasoning, agentic workflows |\n| `llama3.1-8b` | 8B | **DEPRECATED May 27, 2026** | Speed-critical chat |\n| `zai-glm-4.7` | 355B | Preview | Agentic coding, tool use |\n| `qwen-3-235b-a22b-instruct-2507` | 235B | Preview / **DEPRECATED May 27, 2026** | Multilingual, coding |\n\n**Critical note:** `llama3.1-8b` and `qwen-3-235b-a22b-instruct-2507` are deprecated as of May 27, 2026 — do not wire these. Tokens Per Second — Benchmarks\n\n| Model | Cerebras Published TPS | Independent / Observed TPS |\n|---|---|---|\n| `gpt-oss-120b` | ~3,000 tok/s (vendor claim) | 1,910 tok/s (Artificial Analysis measured) |\n| `llama3.1-8b` | ~2,200 tok/s | ~1,800 tok/s |\n| `zai-glm-4.7` | ~1,000–1,700 tok/s | ~1,000 tok/s typical |\n| `qwen-3-235b-a22b-instruct-2507` | ~1,400 tok/s | — |\n\n**Caveat:** Observed real-world TPS under rate limiting and free-tier throttling is significantly lower. One investigative review found bursts of 500 tok/s followed by throttle floors, with sustained throughput sometimes below 100 tok/s on long outputs. Vendor numbers are peak burst, not sustained.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Free Tier Rate Limits (Exact Numbers from Official Docs)\n\n| Limit Type | Free Tier Value |\n|---|---|\n| Requests per minute (RPM) | **5 RPM** |\n| Tokens per minute (TPM) | **30,000 TPM** |\n| Tokens per hour (TPH) | **1,000,000 TPH** |\n| Tokens per day (TPD) | **1,000,000 TPD** |\n\nThese limits apply **per model** at the **organization level** (not per API key or per user).\n\nContext window on free tier: `gpt-oss-120b` is capped at **65k tokens** (131k on paid). Output capped at **32k tokens** (40k on paid).\n\nFor comparison, paid Developer tier gets 1M TPM / 1K RPM for `gpt-oss-120b` — roughly 33x the RPM and 33x the TPM.\n\n---\n\n### 4. API Format\n\n- **OpenAI-compatible:** Yes, drop-in with the OpenAI SDK\n- **Base URL:** `https://api.cerebras.ai/v1`\n- **Auth:** `Authorization: Bearer &lt;CEREBRAS_API_KEY&gt;`\n- **Supported endpoints:** `/chat/completions`, `/completions`, models list\n- **Non-standard parameters:** Pass via `extra_body` when using the OpenAI client (e.g., `reasoning_effort`)\n- **SDKs available:** Official Python SDK (`cerebras-cloud-sdk`) and Node SDK (`@cerebras/cerebras_cloud_sdk`)\n\n**Known compatibility quirk:** The `system` role maps to `developer`-level instructions internally for `gpt-oss-120b`, which can cause different behavior than the same prompt on OpenAI. For `zai-glm-4.7`, tool call format is also slightly non-standard in some edge cases.\n\n---\n\n### 5. Known Issues and Reliability Problems\n\n**Rate limiting behavior:**\n- The rate limiter **estimates token consumption upfront using `max_completion_tokens`**, not actual tokens generated. If you set a high max_tokens ceiling, it reserves that full allocation against your TPM budget even if the model only generates 200 tokens. This is the #1 cause of premature 429s on free tier.\n- Pattern: 10–20 second speed bursts, then hard throttle until the minute window resets.\n- Workaround: Set `max_tokens` conservatively (e.g., 4096 or 8192, not 32768) to avoid burning TPM budget on headroom.\n- Both SDKs have built-in exponential backoff retry on 429 by default.\n\n**Tool hallucination (`gpt-oss-120b`):** Model may invoke tools not in the prompt. Reprompt with correction if this occurs.\n\n**Free-tier TPM temporarily reduced** (per docs): \"Due to high demand, free-tier rate limits have been temporarily reduced for `zai-glm-4.7` and `gpt-oss-120b`.\" The 30K TPM figure reflects this reduced state.\n\n**Reliability incidents:** Cerebras Code (consumer product) had documented outages and billing errors (Pro vs Max tier mismatch with no compensation). API-tier reliability appears better, but is not SLA-backed on free tier.\n\n**Context cap on free tier:** The 65k context cap on `gpt-oss-120b` free tier is a significant constraint for agentic code tasks that pass large file contexts.\n\n---\n\n### 6. Best Single Model for Fast T1 Code Generation\n\n**Recommendation: `gpt-oss-120b`**\n\nReasons:\n- Highest sustained TPS of the surviving non-deprecated models (~1,900 tok/s independently measured, ~3,000 vendor peak)\n- Production status (not preview) — more stable than `zai-glm-4.7` which is still marked preview\n- Explicit code + reasoning + tool-call support\n- Near parity with o4-mini on coding benchmarks (AIME 2025 equal-first)\n- Free tier: 65k context, 32k output, 30K TPM, 5 RPM, 1M TPD\n- Supports `reasoning_effort` parameter passthrough (`\"low\"`, `\"medium\"`, `\"high\"`) — set `\"low\"` for T1 speed\n\n**When to prefer `zai-glm-4.7` instead:** If your tasks are multi-step agentic (plan + tool call + reflect loops) and you can tolerate preview-tier instability. It outperforms `gpt-oss-120b` on SWEBench, τ²bench, and LiveCodeBench per Cerebras's own data. TPS is lower (~1,000) but the output quality on agentic coding tasks may justify it.\n\n**Config for your T1 router:**\n```json\n{\n  \"provider\": \"cerebras\",\n  \"model\": \"gpt-oss-120b\",\n  \"base_url\": \"https://api.cerebras.ai/v1\",\n  \"max_tokens\": 4096,\n  \"reasoning_effort\": \"low\",\n  \"retry_on_429\": true,\n  \"fallback\": \"groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile\"\n}\n```\n\nKeep `max_tokens` at 4096 (not higher) to avoid burning your 30K TPM budget on token headroom the rate limiter pre-reserves.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [Rate Limits - Cerebras Inference](https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/support/rate-limits)\n- [OpenAI Compatibility - Cerebras Inference](https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/resources/openai)\n- [OpenAI GPT OSS - Cerebras Inference](https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/models/openai-oss)\n- [GLM-4.7: Frontier intelligence at record speed - Cerebras Blog](https://www.cerebras.ai/blog/glm-4-7)\n- [Cerebras Free Tier Limits: 1M Tokens/Day benchmarks - Adam Holter](https://adam.holter.com/cerebras-opens-a-free-1m-tokens-per-day-inference-tier-and-ccerebras-now-offers-free-inference-with-1m-tokens-per-day-real-speed-benchmarks-show-2600-tokens-sec-on-llama4scout-here-are-the-actual-n/)\n- [Down and out with Cerebras Code - InfoWorld](https://www.infoworld.com/article/4055909/down-and-out-with-cerebras-code.html)\n- [gpt-oss-120b - API Pricing &amp; Benchmarks - OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai/openai/gpt-oss-120b)\n- [gpt-oss-120b provider benchmarks - Artificial Analysis](https://artificialanalysis.ai/models/gpt-oss-120b/providers)\n- [Cerebras Pricing](https://www.cerebras.ai/pricing)\n- [Tokens per minute limit exceeded - opencode GitHub issue](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/issues/7463)</result>\n<usage><total_tokens>28873</total_tokens><tool_uses>19</tool_uses><duration_ms>129750</duration_ms></usage>\n</task-notification>"

Prior Run 11 figure of ~1,900 t/s is accurate and if anything conservative — it's faster on newer models.\n\n**Free tier limits (from live docs):**\n- 1,000,000 tokens/day\n- 1,000,000 tokens/hour\n- 30,000 tokens/minute\n- **5 RPM** (requests per minute) — this is the binding constraint for agents\n\n**Active models as of May 25, 2026:**\n- `gpt-oss-120b` — fastest, best for code reasoning\n- `zai-glm-4.7` — active (GLM 4.6 deprecated)\n- `llama3.1-8b` — deprecating May 27, 2026\n- `qwen-3-235b-a22b-instruct-2507` — deprecating May 27, 2026\n\n**Critical warning:** Llama-3.3-70B, Llama-4-Scout, Llama-4-Maverick, qwen-3-coder-480b, deepseek-r1-distill-llama-70b are all **already deprecated** on Cerebras. The prior Run 11 reference to \"Llama4Scout at 2,600 t/s\" was real but that model has since been pulled. **GPT-OSS-120B is the live flagship.**\n\n---\n\n### Reliability Red Flag\n\nThe HN thread (\"Tell HN: Avoid Cerebras if you are a founder\") surfaced a real structural problem: Cerebras rotates production models **2-3 times per year with minimal notice** because their wafer-scale chips cannot multi-tenant like GPUs — the entire chip is dedicated to one model at a time, so supporting more models means physically swapping what's loaded. This forces churn.\n\nConfirmed deprecation pattern in 2026 alone: qwen-3-32b and llama-3.3-70b gone February 2026, llama-4-scout and llama-4-maverick gone by May 2026, two more models gone May 27. That is 6 deprecations in ~4 months.\n\n**Practical impact for OB-1:** The agent is already wired to GPT-OSS-120B (Run 12 confirms this). That model appears stable for now (Cerebras partnered with OpenAI to run it, which gives it more staying power than community models). But any model name hardcoded into kilo.json or agent configs should be treated as potentially deprecated on 60-90 day cycles.\n\n---\n\n### Free Tier Comparison for Agents Running Dozens of Calls/Day\n\n| Provider | Daily Token Cap | RPM | Practical Agent Calls/Day |\n|----------|----------------|-----|--------------------------|\n| **Cerebras** | 1,000,000 | **5** | ~5,000 calls @ 200 tokens avg — but **only 5 calls/minute burst** |\n| **Groq** | ~6K TPM / 1K RPD | 30 | 1,000 requests/day hard cap |\n| **SambaNova** | $5 credit (expires 30d) | ~10-20 | Not truly free ongoing |\n\nThe 5 RPM cap on Cerebras is the real bottleneck for an agent. At 5 calls/minute, running \"dozens of times per day\" is fine — 5 RPM = 300 calls/hour, 4,300+ calls/day. That is sufficient for OB-1 T1 grunt work. The 1M token/day limit at ~500 tokens/call average gives ~2,000 calls before hitting the daily ceiling.\n\n**Groq's actual limit (verified):** 1,000 requests/day + 6,000 tokens/minute. For short coding tasks under 2K tokens output, that daily request cap runs out fast in a heavy agent loop.\n\n---\n\n### Recommendation\n\n**Single best combo: Cerebras + GPT-OSS-120B**\n\n- Speed: 1,900-3,000 t/s — no other free provider is close\n- Daily tokens: 1M free, no credit card\n- Code quality: GPT-OSS-120B benchmarks at frontier reasoning level for coding/math\n- TTFT: ~0.49s — sub-500ms first token\n- For tasks under 2K output tokens: at full speed a response completes in under 1 second\n\n**Groq as fallback, not co-primary:** Groq's 1K RPD hard cap makes it unsuitable as a primary for an agent running dozens of calls/day. It burns out. Use it as the fallback when Cerebras is rate-limited (5 RPM burst) or when a specific model is needed.\n\n**Validation of Run 11 findings:** The ~1,900 TPS figure is confirmed accurate (Artificial Analysis independently clocked 1,927 t/s). What is known:\n\n- **DeepSeek V4 Flash** (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash:free`) — MoE architecture with only 13B active params per forward pass. Consistently cited as the **fastest of the quality free models** due to MoE efficiency.\n- **GPT-OSS 20B** (`openai/gpt-oss-20b:free`) — Small model, fastest raw throughput but lower quality.\n- **Qwen3-Coder** (`qwen/qwen3-coder:free`) — Large (480B params) but MoE keeps it snappy. Slower than DS V4 Flash in practice.\n- **DeepSeek R1** / **Qwen3-235B** — Slowest of the free set due to reasoning overhead and size.\n- **Llama 3.3 70B** — Dense model, moderate speed.\n\nCommunity consensus ranking for speed: `gpt-oss-20b` &gt; `deepseek-v4-flash` &gt; `gpt-oss-120b` ≈ `qwen3-coder` &gt; `llama-3.3-70b` &gt; `qwen3-235b` &gt; `deepseek-r1`.\n\nNo hard TPS numbers are published for the free tier endpoints. OpenRouter's benchmark pages show paid endpoints only.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Rate Limits\n\n| Condition | RPM | RPD |\n|---|---|---|\n| No credits on account | 20 req/min | 50 req/day |\n| $10+ credits purchased | 20 req/min | 1,000 req/day |\n\n- No documented **tokens-per-minute** or **tokens-per-day** cap — limits are request-count based only.\n- Limits are **shared across all free models** on the account, not per-model.\n- Exceeding limits returns HTTP 429. Resets daily.\n- The $10 top-up (which you don't have to spend — it just has to exist) unlocks 1,000 RPD. Community calls this \"mandatory for any real dev work.\"\n- Groq comparison: Groq free tier is 30-60 RPM vs OpenRouter's 20 RPM, with no daily cap on many models.\n\n---\n\n### 4. State-of-the-art for free. Best quality.\n2. **`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash:free`** — 284B MoE, 1M context, strong reasoning + coding, fastest of the quality models. Less community data available.\n4. **`poolside/laguna-m.1:free`** — Poolside's flagship, 131K context, coding-focused.\n5. **`deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3-0324:free`** — Reliable multi-step instruction following, clean tool schema adherence. API Format\n\nOpenRouter is **fully OpenAI-compatible**. Drop-in replacement via base URL swap.\n\n**Base URL:** `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`\n\n**Endpoint:** `POST /chat/completions`\n\n**Auth:** `Authorization: Bearer &lt;OPENROUTER_API_KEY&gt;`\n\n**Critical:** Always include `:free` suffix in model ID. Without it, a paid account gets billed.\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $OPENROUTER_KEY\" \\\n  -d '{\n    \"model\": \"deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash:free\",\n    \"messages\": [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"...\"}]\n  }'\n```\n\n**Python (OpenAI SDK):**\n```python\nfrom openai import OpenAI\nclient = OpenAI(\n    base_url=\"https://openrouter.ai/api/v1\",\n    api_key=\"&lt;OPENROUTER_KEY&gt;\"\n)\nresponse = client.chat.completions.create(\n    model=\"deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash:free\",\n    messages=[{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"...\"}]\n)\n```\n\nOptional tracking headers: `HTTP-Referer` and `X-Title` (don't affect routing).\n\nThe `openrouter/free` model ID auto-routes to an available free model — useful as a fallback when you don't care which model answers.\n\n---\n\n### 6. Reliability Issues\n\n**Hard facts:**\n- 3 documented outages in 8 months (Aug 2025, Feb 2026, one other). Each 35-50 minutes. ~99.96% uptime overall.\n- No SLA. No uptime guarantee at any tier.\n- During outages, errors surface as 401 (auth-looking) rather than 5xx — debugging is confusing.\n\n**Free-tier-specific problems:**\n- 429s are frequent on popular models (especially DeepSeek variants) during peak hours — free queues deprioritized vs paid.\n- Model IDs rotate. A working `:free` model can disappear from the free tier with no notice. Community reports this happens \"within weeks\" for newer models.\n- Tool/function-calling on free models: hallucination of tool schemas is documented. Only `deepseek-chat-v3-0324:free` and `qwen3-235b:free` are consistently cited as reliable for structured tool use.\n- Multi-turn context accumulation degrades quality on free tiers faster than paid (likely due to truncation behavior).\n\n**For T1 dispatch router — design implications:**\n- Must implement exponential backoff on 429.\n- Must maintain a fallback chain: primary → secondary → tertiary free model → escalate to paid.\n- Do not hardcode a single `:free` model ID — the router needs at least 2-3 candidates.\n- The 50 RPD limit (no credits) will hit immediately in any real dev session. The $10 top-up is required for 1,000 RPD.\n- Groq is a better pure-speed free alternative for simpler T1 tasks (30-60 RPM, no daily cap on Llama 3.3 70B).\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [Free AI Models on OpenRouter | OpenRouter](https://openrouter.ai/collections/free-models)\n- [OpenRouter Free Models: All 28 Listed (May 2026)](https://costgoat.com/pricing/openrouter-free-models)\n- [Best Free Models on OpenRouter 2026](https://www.teamday.ai/blog/best-free-ai-models-openrouter-2026)\n- [API Rate Limits | OpenRouter Documentation](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api/reference/limits)\n- [OpenRouter Rate Limits – Zendesk](https://openrouter.zendesk.com/hc/en-us/articles/39501163636379-OpenRouter-Rate-Limits-What-You-Need-to-Know)\n- [Is OpenRouter Reliable? {
  "role": "user",
  "content": "<task-notification>\n<task-id>ada0f124e4aa62692</task-id>\n<tool-use-id>toolu_01CR25ikwTmaCJdF4mypqef6</tool-use-id>\n<output-file>C:\\Users\\mrmic\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\d--OB-1-CANONICAL\\6e1a4098-85ce-4cf8-855f-e54e69c208b8\\tasks\\ada0f124e4aa62692.output</output-file>\n<status>completed</status>\n<summary>Agent \"T2-B: Kilo custom provider config — what's blocking it\" completed</summary>\n<result>Based on extensive research across Kilo docs, GitHub issues, Cerebras/Groq docs, and community sources, here is a complete findings report:\n\n---\n\n## Kilo AI Custom Provider Config — Research Findings\n\n### 1. Can you configure Cerebras/Groq as a free/default model in kilo.json?\n\n**Yes, you can set them as the default model — but not as a \"free\" model in the kilo-auto sense.**\n\nBoth Cerebras and Groq are first-class named providers in Kilo (not \"custom\" providers). The config format in `kilo.jsonc` is:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://app.kilo.ai/config.json\",\n  \"model\": \"cerebras/zai-glm-4.7\",  // global default\n  \"provider\": {\n    \"cerebras\": {\n      \"options\": {\n        \"apiKey\": \"{env:CEREBRAS_API_KEY}\"\n      }\n    },\n    \"groq\": {\n      \"options\": {\n        \"apiKey\": \"{env:GROQ_API_KEY}\"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nPer-agent model config:\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"agent\": {\n    \"code\": {\n      \"model\": \"cerebras/zai-glm-4.7\",\n      \"reasoningEffort\": \"medium\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe top-level `\"model\"` key sets the global default. The per-agent `\"model\"` key overrides it per agent type. `reasoningEffort` is passed through to the provider as a raw option.\n\n### 2. Known bugs and limitations blocking free-tier providers\n\nSeveral confirmed issues found:\n\n**a. `kilo/kilo-auto/free` \"Model not found\" errors (Issues #7419, #7894)**\nThe `kilo-auto/free` virtual model ID was not registered in the `/api/gateway/models` endpoint, causing clients that validate model IDs to throw `UnknownError: Model not found: kilo/kilo-auto/free`. This was marked closed/duplicate but root cause was the virtual model not being in the catalog endpoint. Issue #6686 (kilo/auto and kilo/auto-free missing from gateway models endpoint) confirms this — it was closed, suggesting a fix was deployed, but the fix was backend-side only.\n\n**b. `gpt-oss-120B` tool call failure via Kilo Gateway (Issue #4444)**\nWhen routing through the Kilo Gateway (`kilo/` prefix), `gpt-oss-120b` fails on tool calls and enters an infinite error loop. Closed as \"not planned\" / stale — meaning this is an **unresolved known blocker** for using the gateway-routed version of gpt-oss. Using Cerebras directly (provider key `cerebras`) bypasses this issue.\n\n**c. Model selection UI reverts to blank (Issue #1216)**\nAcross all providers, when selecting a model in the settings UI, the dropdown reverts to blank state, blocking multimodal and model-specific features. Closed as \"not planned\" — **never fixed**. This means UI-based provider setup for Cerebras/Groq is unreliable; direct `kilo.jsonc` editing is required.\n\n**d. Model switching broken when default set in kilo.jsonc (Issue #9050)**\nWhen a per-agent model is configured in `kilo.jsonc`, switching models in the Kilo CLI does not take effect — the agent keeps using the configured default. PR #9625 was assigned. Bug reported April 2026, status unclear as of research date.\n\n**e. Forced provider modal blocks config import on first launch (Issue #1389)**\nOn first launch, a blocking \"Select a provider\" modal prevents importing a pre-existing `kilo.jsonc`. Closed as \"not planned\" / stale. Workaround: temporarily configure Ollama with placeholder values to dismiss the modal, then import config.\n\n**f. Custom provider model deletion fails (Issue #8316)**\nIn v7.1.2, models added to custom providers via the UI Settings cannot be deleted — they persist after clicking the trash icon. Unresolved.\n\n### 3. What does kilo.json provider config actually support?\n\nFrom the live schema at `https://app.kilo.ai/config.json`, the `ProviderConfig` shape is:\n\n```\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.options.apiKey\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.options.baseURL\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.options.timeout\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.options.chunkTimeout\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.models.&lt;model_key&gt;.id\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.models.&lt;model_key&gt;.name\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.models.&lt;model_key&gt;.tool_call\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.models.&lt;model_key&gt;.limit.context\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.models.&lt;model_key&gt;.limit.output\nprovider.&lt;provider_id&gt;.whitelist / blacklist\n```\n\nThe `kilo-auto/free` routing is **server-controlled** and not configurable. The docs confirm: \"the mapping is updated server-side — you always get the best free option without having to track what's currently available.\" You cannot point `kilo-auto/free` at a custom provider. It routes to whichever free models Kilo's server currently maps it to (historically MiniMax M2.5 80% + StepFun 20%, now updated to xAI Grok Code Fast + NVIDIA Nemotron + Arcee Trinity).\n\n### 4. Setting Cerebras as the default for new chats\n\n**Yes, this works.** Set `\"model\": \"cerebras/zai-glm-4.7\"` at the root of your `kilo.jsonc`. The model precedence is:\n\n```\nSession override → Last picked per agent → Per-agent config → Global config → Auto Free\n```\n\nSo a global config default sits at level 4. If the user has previously picked a different model in a session (level 2), that persists and overrides the config default. This is the primary UX friction — the \"last picked\" memory overrides config.\n\nThe specific Cerebras model IDs available in Kilo: `zai-glm-4.6`, `zai-glm-4.7`, `gpt-oss-120b` (default set by Kilo in changelog). The format for kilo.jsonc is `cerebras/zai-glm-4.7` or `cerebras/gpt-oss-120b`.\n\nGroq models: `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `llama-3.1-70b-versatile`, `llama-3.1-8b-instant`, `mixtral-8x7b-32768`, `moonshotai/kimi-k2-instruct`.\n\n### 5. VS Code extension conflicts and config problems\n\n- **Config file location**: Global = `C:\\Users\\&lt;username&gt;\\.config\\kilo\\kilo.jsonc` on Windows. Project-level = `kilo.jsonc` in project root or `.kilo/kilo.jsonc`.\n- **API keys in config**: Never put raw API keys in `kilo.jsonc` — use `{env:CEREBRAS_API_KEY}` syntax. Raw keys will work but trigger VS Code secret scanning.\n- **UI vs file config**: The Settings UI reads/writes the same `kilo.jsonc` files. Due to the UI model selection bug (Issue #1216, closed as not planned), direct file editing is more reliable.\n- **`reasoningEffort` passthrough**: Confirmed working — any key not in the documented agent schema is passed through to the provider. Cerebras `gpt-oss-120b` supports `reasoningEffort: \"low\" | \"medium\" | \"high\"`. Groq supports the same. Works in both the `agent` config and as a top-level provider option.\n- **OpenAI-compatible fallback**: Issue #6315 confirmed that `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` is the default fallback, so any provider with a `baseURL` pointing to an OpenAI-compatible endpoint will work even without a named provider key.\n\n### The Core Blocker for Your Use Case (ROMAN Run T1 agent)\n\nThe most likely blocker is the **\"last picked per agent\" memory at precedence level 2 overriding kilo.jsonc defaults (level 4)**. If any prior session had a different model selected for the agent slot, that memory persists and ignores the config file. The fix is to manually select Cerebras in the UI once to set the \"last picked\" memory, then the config will be stable. Issue #9050 (April 2026) also shows that even explicit kilo.jsonc per-agent model config can fail to apply in the CLI when model switching is involved — PR #9625 was filed for this.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [Custom Models — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/code-with-ai/agents/custom-models)\n- [Free and Budget Models — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/code-with-ai/agents/free-and-budget-models)\n- [Auto Model — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/code-with-ai/agents/auto-model)\n- [Using Kilo for Free — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/getting-started/using-kilo-for-free)\n- [Model Selection Guide — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/code-with-ai/agents/model-selection)\n- [Settings — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/getting-started/settings)\n- [AI Providers — kilo.ai](https://kilo.ai/docs/ai-providers)\n- [Gateway Models &amp; Let me compile everything.\n\n---\n\nHere is the full research report with hard facts only, no speculation.\n\n---\n\n## Kilo `kilo-auto/free` — State as of May 2026\n\n### 1. What IS `kilo-auto/free` — how does it work?\n\nIt is entirely **server-side**. The client sends the model ID `kilo-auto/free` to `api.kilo.ai`. The Kilo Gateway backend resolves it to a specific free model at runtime. The client has no visibility into which model it actually gets. Routing is \"selected deterministically based on the session (or user/IP)\" from a \"curated set of available free models.\" Kilo's own architecture docs confirm: \"the actual model-to-mode mappings are not hardcoded in the client — they're served dynamically from the Kilo API.\"\n\nKey code fact from `packages/kilo-gateway/src/api/constants.ts` in the live repo:\n\n```typescript\nexport const DEFAULT_MODEL = \"kilo-auto/free\"\nexport const DEFAULT_FREE_MODEL = \"kilo-auto/free\"\n```\n\nBoth authenticated and anonymous users default to this. New users (as of May 11, 2026, issue #10151 merged) get `kilo-auto/free` as their default model out of the box.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Was the MiniMax 429 / StepFun 404 breakage still active in May 2026?\n\n**The original 80/20 split (MiniMax M2.5 / StepFun Step-3.5-Flash) was the official routing as recently as April 17, 2026.** This is confirmed by GitHub issue #9126 (merged April 17), which explicitly documented: \"Free: documented the 80/20 split between `minimax-m2.5:free` and `stepfun/step-3.5-flash:free`.\"\n\nThe 404 on StepFun was valid — Step-3.5-Flash was removed from OpenRouter's free tier. The 429 on MiniMax was real as well.\n\nAs of April 22, 2026 (issue #9395, closed), Kilo updated the docs: \"Free: Removed stale hardcoded 80/20 split; routing is now dynamic and selected server-side from available free models.\" This confirms the MiniMax/StepFun split was **explicitly deprecated and removed** from the routing logic.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Has Kilo shipped a fix?\n\nYes. The fix came in stages:\n\n- **April 22, 2026** — Issue #9395 closed: gateway docs updated, 80/20 MiniMax/StepFun split removed, replaced with fully dynamic server-side selection from \"available free models.\"\n- **April 29, 2026** — Issue #9699 closed: further sync of `kilo-auto/*` tier mappings with gateway implementation.\n- **May 11, 2026** — Issue #10151 merged: default model changed to `kilo-auto/free` for all new users.\n- **May 11, 2026** — Issue #10161 merged: VS Code extension now shows a sign-in nudge for signed-out users on Auto Free.\n\nThere is no single \"fix\" commit — instead, they transitioned from the broken hardcoded split to a fully dynamic server-side model pool that they update independently of the client.\n\n---\n\n### 4. If you reinstall Kilo fresh (latest v7.3.1), does free auto mode work?\n\nThe current extension version is **v7.3.1** (released May 19, 2026). Fresh install defaults to `kilo-auto/free`. The `kilo-auto/free` model ID is now properly discoverable via the API (issue #6686 was closed April 15, 2026 with the comment \"The models are in the response from the backend now\").\n\n**However**, two open issues (#9789 and #9932, both from early May 2026) still report `UnknownError: Model not found: kilo/kilo-auto/free.` These are unresolved and have zero comments — no Kilo team response. The error pattern suggests it may be specific to certain client configurations or older cached provider metadata, not a universal failure.\n\nThere is also issue #10364 (open, May 18, 2026): \"Nemotron 3 Nano Omni 30B (free) infinite loop with malformed XML tool calls\" — which indicates at least one of the free models currently in the pool has a tool-call bug.\n\n---\n\n### 5. What models does `kilo-auto/free` currently use?\n\n**Kilo deliberately does not publish the current free model list.** From the live docs (confirmed from the repo source `models-and-providers.md`):\n\n&gt; \"The resolved model is selected dynamically per session from a curated set of available free models; the mapping updates server-side as free model availability shifts.\"\n\nThe **separately-addressable** free models (not auto-free, but directly usable with their IDs) listed in the docs as of May 2026 are:\n\n| Model ID | Provider |\n|---|---|\n| `x-ai/grok-code-fast-1:optimized:free` | xAI |\n| `nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free` | NVIDIA |\n| `arcee-ai/trinity-large-thinking:free` | Arcee AI |\n| `openrouter/free` | OpenRouter passthrough |\n\nWhat is NOT in the auto-free pool anymore: MiniMax M2.5:free (usage issues) and StepFun Step-3.5-Flash:free (delisted from OpenRouter). Both were removed from the hardcoded routing in April 2026.\n\nThe free auto pool **likely includes** grok-code-fast-1, NVIDIA Nemotron, possibly Gemma 4 variants via OpenRouter's free tier, but Kilo does not commit to a specific list. Note: issue #9269 (open) is a feature request to formally deprecate `arcee-ai/trinity-large-thinking:free` from the pool.\n\nThe `kilo-auto/small` tier (used for background tasks like session titles/summaries) is resolved to `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it:free` for free accounts — this one IS documented.\n\n---\n\n### Summary for your specific use case (kilo.json T1 agent)\n\nThe April 2026 breakage (MiniMax 429 + StepFun 404) has been resolved at the routing level — those models are no longer in the auto-free pool. The current v7.3.1 extension ships with `kilo-auto/free` as the default. The model-not-found errors from early May 2026 (issues #9789, #9932) are still open and unresolved, suggesting the identifier can still fail in some edge cases. If your `kilo.json` was configured with the old MiniMax/StepFun model IDs directly (rather than the `kilo-auto/free` virtual ID), those would still fail — those model IDs are gone from the free tier. If you're using `kilo-auto/free` as the ID, it should work on a fresh install with v7.3.1, with the caveat that you may get NVIDIA Nemotron (which has tool-call bugs per issue #10364) as the resolved model.\n\nSources:\n- [Kilo Auto Model Docs](https://kilo.ai/docs/code-with-ai/agents/auto-model)\n- [Kilo Models &amp; Providers Docs](https://kilo.ai/docs/gateway/models-and-providers)\n- [Using Kilo for Free](https://kilo.ai/docs/getting-started/using-kilo-for-free)\n- [Kilo-Org/kilocode GitHub](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode)\n- [Issue #9126 — docs: update kilo-auto model mappings (April 17, 2026)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/9126)\n- [Issue #9395 — docs: update kilo-auto model routing (April 22, 2026)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/9395)\n- [Issue #9699 — sync kilo-auto tier mappings (April 29, 2026)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/9699)\n- [Issue #10151 — change default model to kilo-auto/free (May 11, 2026)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/10151)\n- [Issue #9789 — Model not found: kilo/kilo-auto/free (May 2, 2026, OPEN)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/9789)\n- [Issue #9932 — Model not found: kilo/kilo-auto/free (May 5, 2026, OPEN)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/9932)\n- [Issue #10364 — Nemotron 3 Nano Omni infinite loop (May 18, 2026, OPEN)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/10364)\n- [Issue #6686 — kilo/auto-free missing from models endpoint (closed April 2026)](https://github.com/Kilo-Org/kilocode/issues/6686)\n- [Step 3.5 Flash is Free in Kilo — blog post](https://blog.kilo.ai/p/step-35-flash-is-free-in-kilo)</result>\n<usage><total_tokens>57520</total_tokens><tool_uses>62</tool_uses><duration_ms>328490</duration_ms></usage>\n</task-notification>"
